> **Correction 2026-10-28 (see the next section).** The NEW-134 row above inferred that `gymsera` came from the host or app-pool environment. For apistaging that was wrong: its own `.env` sets it. dotenv trims leading spaces, so the line ` PLATFORM_DB_NAME=gymsera` counts. A search anchored at the start of the line (`^PLATFORM_DB_NAME`) misses it, which may also be why production's `.env` looked as if it had no such line. The code change in `05a9472` stands: it prints where the name came from, whichever source that is. The expected production boot line in the check above may therefore read `(PLATFORM_DB_NAME from .env)`. Either source is fine on production; what matters is that apistaging never resolves to `gymsera`.

### NEW-127 / NEW-134 root cause 2026-10-28: the staging API wrote into the production platform database (Claude Code Opus 5.5)

Owner evidence (production server, 2026-10-28): apistaging's `.env` sets `PLATFORM_DB_NAME=gymsera` on line 18, with a leading space. That is the same platform database production uses. Both sites also set `TENANT_DB_HOST=209.209.42.64`, the same tenant database server. So the apistaging API, which runs on the same server, read and wrote the production platform database and could create tenant databases on the production tenant server. The owner has deployed `b87907c` (PR #178: NEW-127 proof, NEW-130 build id, NEW-131/NEW-134 staging guard, NEW-133) to production, set production's `APP_URL` to `https://api.gymsera.com`, and stopped apistaging.

**Root cause of NEW-127 (the 10/24 tenant without `schema_migrations`).** Two APIs with different code shared one platform database. Production's own code on 10/24 could not leave that state: main since `d7d4179` runs every migration, and since `d326909` it records `provisioning_state`. The September-build behaviour (24-table sync, no migrations, ACTIVE, no `provisioning_state`; matched on `f083c0f`) was therefore produced by a second API process on the same database. apistaging is the one now proven to have been connected to it. Nothing stopped it: before NEW-131 nothing compared a staging site's database with production's, and the NEW-131 guard as first written assumed production was `gymsera_platform` (NEW-134).

| | Status |
|---|---|
| apistaging's `.env` names the production platform database (`gymsera`, line 18, leading space) and the production tenant server (`209.209.42.64`) | **Proven** (owner, server `.env` files) |
| dotenv reads a line with a leading space | **Proven** (dotenv 16.6.1, the installed version: `dotenv.parse(' PLATFORM_DB_NAME=gymsera')` gives `gymsera`) |
| GYM-4814C134 was built by code from before `d7d4179`, and production's main on 10/24 could not build it | **Proven** (section "NEW-127 production evidence 2026-10-27"; red 6/8 on `f083c0f`) |
| The guard in `b87907c` refuses to start a staging site on `gymsera` | **Proven** (`new-131` and `new-134` tests; production now runs it) |
| The request that approved GYM-4814C134 (2026-10-24 10:43:44 server time) went to apistaging | **Not proven.** There was no build id or platform database name in any log then (NEW-130 came after), and the API log folder had no new file since 2026-10-16 (NEW-123). Only the IIS logs of both sites for `POST /api/v1/admin/tenants/<id>/approve` around that time can show which site served it |
| Which code apistaging was running on 10/24 | **Not proven.** It fits a build cut between 2026-09-15 and `d7d4179`, but nothing recorded it. Its folder's `git log -1` today shows its current code, not the code on 10/24 unless nothing was pulled since |
| How the CMS reached apistaging (a CMS build with `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL=https://apistaging.gymsera.com`, or a person using the staging CMS) | **Not proven.** NEW-132 shows such a build was possible |
| What else the staging API wrote into production (other approvals, settings, subscriptions, jobs and crons run twice) | **Not known.** The database holds test data only (R-19), so this is not a data-loss incident; the 30 other tenants pass `run-tenant-migrations.js --check` |
| The iisnode `watchedFiles` and wrong-folder leads in the 2026-10-27 section | **Not needed** to explain 10/24 and not proven. They remain possible ways for a site to run stale code; the NEW-130 boot line and `/health` `build.commit` now show it |

**What prevents a repeat.** In code (deployed in `b87907c`): the staging guard (NEW-131, NEW-134) refuses to start a site whose `NODE_ENV`, `GYMSERA_ENV` or `APP_URL` says staging while its platform database is production's (`gymsera`, or `PRODUCTION_PLATFORM_DB_NAME`). The boot line and `/health` show the commit and the platform database with its source (NEW-130, NEW-134). In process: `docs/runbooks/production-environments.md` (new). Staging gets its own platform and tenant databases before it is turned on, and `.env` files are never copied between sites.

### NEW-135 2026-10-28: staging refuses production's tenant database server (Claude Code Opus 5.5)

Owner decision 2026-10-28: production's platform database is on `localhost`; its tenant server is the `TENANT_DB_HOST` in production's `.env`, `209.209.42.64`. Refuse to start a staging site whose resolved tenant host equals the production tenant host; `PRODUCTION_TENANT_DB_HOST` overrides it, with that value as the default. Print host names only. **Today staging and production use the same tenant server, so staging stays off until it has its own tenant server, or its own users and databases (see the note under the table).**

| Issue | Root cause | Pattern reused | Fix | Regression test | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **NEW-135** (P1) the guard did not check the tenant server | `environment-guard.js#checkEnvironment` compared only `PLATFORM_DB_NAME`. Tenant databases are named `gymsera_<tenant code>` (`tenant-provisioning.service.js:86-89`) whatever the platform database is, so a staging site with its own platform name but production's `TENANT_DB_HOST` could create and use databases on the production tenant server. On 2026-10-28 both sites' `.env` had `TENANT_DB_HOST=209.209.42.64` | NEW-131 / NEW-134 guard, `envSource` (where a value came from), the NEW-130 boot line | `database.config.js#resolveTenantDbHost` returns `{ host, source }` by the rule provisioning uses (`TENANT_DB_HOST`, else `PLATFORM_DB_HOST`, else `127.0.0.1`); `getTenantDbConfig` now calls it, so the guard and provisioning cannot disagree. The guard refuses staging when that host equals `PRODUCTION_TENANT_DB_HOST` (default `209.209.42.64`), compared as host names (case, spaces and a trailing dot ignored); a refusal names every problem (platform database, tenant server, or both) with its source. Same error code `STAGING_ON_PRODUCTION_DB`. `check-environment.js` prints `tenant database server:` and `production tenant database server:` with their sources. Boot line adds `tenantDbHost=<host> (<source>)`. `.env.example` documents the override | `tests/regression/new-135-staging-tenant-host-guard.test.js` (13) | Red 9/13 (the 4 "allowed" cases already passed), green 13/13. Full suite in band with the CI-style env from a clean worktree without `.env`: 239/239 suites, 2459/2459 tests. be `10d6243`. **VERIFIED in production 2026-10-28** (section "Phase 7 production verification 2026-10-28") |

Limits (not fixed, recorded): the guard compares host names, not machines. Another name or IP for the same MySQL server (for example `127.0.0.1` if `209.209.42.64` is this machine) passes it, so the runbook rule still applies. Staging with its own MySQL users and databases on `209.209.42.64` is also refused; allowing it would need a new owner decision on how the guard should tell the cases apart (for example an explicit allow setting), which is not built. A different `TENANT_DB_PORT` on the same host is refused too.

Owner check after deploying `10d6243`: production's boot line shows `tenantDbHost=209.209.42.64 (TENANT_DB_HOST from .env)` and starts. Before turning apistaging on again, `node src\scripts\check-environment.js` in its folder must print `OK`, with a tenant database server that is not `209.209.42.64`.

### Drop tools take names only from `tenants.db_name` 2026-10-28 (R-79, Claude Code Opus 5.5)

The owner asked that no tenant database name is inferred, and that the launch wipe and the orphan-database drop refuse a name the `tenants` table does not hold (R-79). Checked both on `docs/new-127-verified` (`83bdf91` + docs).

| Issue | Root cause | Pattern reused | Fix | Test | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **NEW-136** (P1, launch blocker) the launch wipe cannot read the real `tenants` table | `platform-reset.service.js:100` ran `` SELECT id, status, `dbName` FROM tenants ``. The Tenant model is `underscored: true`, so the column is `db_name` (checked on the local MySQL 5.7 schema; every `docs/sql` check uses `db_name`). On the real schema the plan step fails `Unknown column 'dbName'`, dry run included, so the wipe could not run (it failed before any delete, so nothing was lost). The test created its own `tenants` table with a `dbName` column, which hid it. Names already came only from that read, never from `tenant_code` | the existing plan / drop loop and its refusals | Reads `id, tenant_code, status, db_name`; the plan line lists `tenantCode` next to `dbName`, so the dry run can be compared with the R-79 query. A tenant with NULL `db_name` is skipped (as before) | `tests/regression/reset-platform-data.test.js` (9): scratch table now has the real column names; new: the plan lists `tenant_code` with `db_name`; a tenant with NULL `db_name` whose naming-rule database exists keeps it | Red 7/9 (`Unknown column 'dbName'`), green 9/9 |
| **NEW-137** (P1) the orphan-database drop worked a name out from `tenant_code` | `orphan-database.service.js:66-71` (`_findTenant`): when no `tenants.db_name` matched, it took every tenant with NULL `db_name` and matched `buildDbName(tenant_code)`. Provisioning writes `db_name` only at its last step (`tenant-provisioning.service.js:733`), so a run that failed earlier leaves exactly that state, and an otherwise eligible tenant (REJECTED > 90 days, no money rows) had its naming-rule database dropped on `--apply --confirm`, a name the table never held. Against R-79 | NEW-34 refusals (`assessOrphanDrop` "no tenant row", dry run default, `--confirm <exact name>`) | `_findTenant` is an exact `tenants.db_name` match only; the fallback and its `buildDbName` / `Op` imports are gone. No match is the existing "no tenant row" refusal, which now says no `tenants.db_name` holds the name. Such a database stays for the owner to decide by hand (`provisioning-failure.md`) | `tests/regression/new-34-reject-during-provisioning-and-orphan-databases.test.js` (24): new case, REJECTED tenant > 90 days with `db_name` NULL and its `gymsera_<tenant code>` database present; dry run not eligible, `--apply --confirm` refused, database kept | Red 1/24 (the dry run mapped it through `tenant_code`), green 24/24 |

That happens with or without this change, and the full suite passes. `docs/sql/tenant-db-name-before-drop.sql` passes `run-sql-check.js` (2 statements, read-only grid) against the local test database. ### Phase 7 Prompt P3: real Google Maps, Places and location 2026-10-28 (R-76, Claude Code Opus 5.5)

Scope given by the owner: audit the Maps keys only (no key created or rotated, R-76), move the mobile key out of `AndroidManifest.xml` and `AppDelegate.swift` into build-time config, and cover the real map picker, city and area search, address autocomplete and "Use current location" in the Become a Host flow and the traveller flow. Failing test first for each bug. Local branches, not pushed: be `fix/p3-google-places` (from `origin/main` `83bdf91`), app `fix/p3-google-maps-places` (from `origin/master` `c5d4d15`).

**Key audit (no key created, none rotated).** One Google key exists in the repos: the mobile Maps client key, the same value in `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:54` and `ios/Runner/AppDelegate.swift:11` (compared by fingerprint, never printed into files). It is not the Firebase key (`GoogleService-Info.plist`, `google-services.json`, `firebase_options.dart` carry a different, public-by-design key). No server key and no web key is in any `.env.example`; the API `.env` has no Maps variable; gymsera_web reads `NEXT_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` (`src/components/features/gym-map.tsx:12`, `location-picker.tsx:216`) without listing it in `.env.example` (NEW-140). The key restrictions themselves are in Google Cloud and were not checked (owner).

**Design.** Search, place details and reverse geocoding go through the API (`/api/v1/places/*`) with the server key from the API env (`GOOGLE_MAPS_SERVER_KEY`), as §2 "Owner setup" of the prompt pack describes ("server key ... used only by the backend for Place Details / Geocoding"). The API upserts every resolved city and area into `cities` / `areas` (by Google place id, else by name ignoring case and extra spaces, so an admin-made city is reused), and answers with our ids, so tenants, branches, discovery filters and `/admin/cities` keep working unchanged. The mobile key only draws maps. Travellers never call Google: reverse-geocoding a traveller's position would create a city for every traveller location (BUG-26 asks for fewer cities, not more).

Tests: backend full suite 241 / 241 suites, 2485 / 2485 tests green (be `fa8b134`) (local Docker MySQL 5.7, `DISABLE_REDIS=true`, in band). Existing tests changed and why: the SEC-06 declared-field fixtures gained `googlePlaceId` on the four routes that now declare it (the fixture is a deliberate drift guard); NEW-126 / NEW-127 tests that pinned the tenant schema target at v19 now derive it from `TARGET_SCHEMA_VERSION` (same assertions; 020 moved the target to v20). p024 skips a missing `cities` / `areas` table (the migration-runner scratch databases have none; found by the first full run). App: `flutter test` 267 / 267 three times; `flutter analyze` 122 findings against 123 on `master`, none new. NEW-110 guard: the traveller position is reset on logout; the Places repository, location service and map builder are listed as app-wide.

**Deploy (backend first, migrations before code).** (1) Backup: `mysqldump <platform_db> cities areas > cities_areas_pre_p024.sql`. (2) `node src/scripts/run-platform-migrations.js --dry-run`, then without the flag (p024). (3) `node src/scripts/run-tenant-migrations.js --dry-run`, then without the flag (020 on every tenant), then `node src/scripts/run-tenant-migrations.js --check` must print OK **before** the new code serves: the Branch model selects `google_place_id`, so a tenant left at v19 fails every branch read (the NEW-127 lesson). (4) Set `GOOGLE_MAPS_SERVER_KEY` in the API `.env` (the new server key from GymsEra's own project, R-76a, restricted to the server IP and to Places API (New) + Geocoding API); optional `GOOGLE_PLACES_REGION_CODES`, `RATE_LIMIT_PLACES_USER_MAX`. (5) Deploy the code; `/health/tenant-schema` must answer 200. (6) Mobile build machine: the new Android key in `android/local.properties` `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY=` and the new iOS key in `ios/Flutter/MapsKey.xcconfig` (R-76a; replace the old key the P3 run copied there) (`docs/MAPS_KEY_SETUP.md` in gyms_era), then the usual `--dart-define=APP_ENV=production` builds. Rollback: p024 and 020 rollback SQL are in the migration comments (previous code first).

Not tested: real Google (tests never reach the network, R-19, and no server key exists locally); the map, GPS and permission dialogs on a device; the iOS app at run time (build settings only); a full Android release build (the missing-key guard was run as a Gradle dry run); staging. Not done: the CMS branch create/edit form and its Playwright test (P3 text; outside the scope the owner gave for this run; carried to P9 as NEW-140).

New: **NEW-138** (P2) `SelectedCity` (`discovery_provider.dart:292-316` on the P3 branch) defaults to `{id: 1, name: 'Karachi'}` when nothing is saved and the city list is empty or fails, and its asynchronous first load can overwrite a city chosen right after start (P6, with BUG-26's "nearest live city"). **NEW-139** (P2) other invented values on traveller screens: stock gym photo fallback (`gym_detail_screen.dart:97-101`), hours "06:00 AM - 11:00 PM" (`gym_detail_screen.dart:108-110`), price "Rs2,500" (`map_search_screen.dart:235`), Near You distance `?? 1.2` km (`home_screen.dart:306,318`), Home header "Karachi" with no city (`home_screen.dart:166`) (P6 / P8 BUG-38 sweep). **NEW-140** (P2) CMS branch form still on our city/area lists; web reads `NEXT_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` without an `.env.example` entry (P9). Risk, not a defect: a host's Google pick creates an active city before it has a live gym, so the traveller city list can grow until P6 (BUG-26, R-77) filters it.

### NEW-144: iOS purchase sync 500 "Cannot find module 'axios'" on production 2026-10-28 (Claude Code Opus 5.5; cross-reference P7a)

Owner evidence: the production error journal shows `POST /api/v1/billing/ios/sync` answering 500 `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` "Cannot find module 'axios'" (required by `src/services/apple-billing.service.js`). Production installs with `npm install --omit=dev`. Branch be `fix/axios-runtime-dependency` from `origin/main` `6b9478b`, not pushed.

| Issue | Root cause (file:line) | Pattern reused | Fix | Test | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **NEW-144** (P0, money: iOS purchases not recorded on production) | `package.json:53` declares `axios` in `devDependencies`; `package-lock.json` marks `axios`, `follow-redirects`, `form-data` and `proxy-from-env` `"dev": true` (and `form-data`'s `asynckit` / `combined-stream` / `delayed-stream` `devOptional`), so `--omit=dev` leaves them out. `apple-billing.service.js:134` (transaction lookup, used by iOS sync and verify) and `:255` (subscription status) require it inside the function, so the server starts and only those calls fail | npm's own `--save-prod`; jest regression test | `axios` moved to `dependencies` at the version already locked (1.20.0, range `^1.20.0`); lockfile updated by npm (`--package-lock-only`), no version change | `tests/regression/runtime-dependencies.test.js` (3): reads every `require` / `import` in `src/`, `server.js`, `app.js` (comments ignored; the vendored `src/socket/socket-bundle.js` excluded: its `bufferutil` / `utf-8-validate` are optional, in try/catch) and fails if a package is not in `dependencies` or the lockfile marks it, or anything it needs (resolved as Node does), dev or devOptional | red 2/3 on `main`, green 3/3 |

**Scan (item 2).** 316 files, 32 packages. Only `axios` is required at run time and not in `dependencies`. Not real: `bufferutil`, `utf-8-validate` (optional, vendored bundle), and two words in comments that a plain text search reads as module names.

**Production-style install (item 4).** `git archive origin/main` → `npm ci --omit=dev --ignore-scripts`, then load each billing module and resolve every package it requires, including requires inside functions: `apple-billing`, `google-play-billing`, `stripe-billing`, `billing-event`, `billing-plan-catalog`, `branch-billing-lock`, `subscription-migration`, `subscription-quota`, `payment` services, `billing` / `billing-plans` controllers and routes, and `app.js`. On `main`: every module and `app.js` load; **`axios` does not resolve** (2 failures, both in `apple-billing.service.js`): the production failure. Every other package those files require resolves, so Google Play verify and the webhook handlers (in the billing controller, routes and services above) load in a production install; only the Apple calls that use axios fail. With the fix: all load and every package resolves.

**Deploy (no migration, no env change).** On the API server: `git pull`, `npm install --omit=dev` (installs axios), `iisreset`. Check: `node -e "require.resolve('axios')"` in the app folder prints a path, then an iOS sandbox purchase or restore on a device answers 200 on `/billing/ios/sync`.

**Open (P7a / owner).** iOS purchases made while sync answered 500 may not be recorded on our side (the app got an error, so no entitlement was granted). After the deploy: check the error journal for each failed `/billing/ios/sync` since axios moved to dev, and have those users run Restore Purchases (or re-sync) on the fixed server; check `billing_events` for the matching transactions. Not checked here (no production database access from this session).

Next prompt: **P4** (`GYMSERA_PRODUCTION_FIX_PROMPTS.md` §5, membership plans).

### Phase 7 Prompt P4: membership plans, visibility, naming, currency 2026-10-29 (R-80, Claude Code Opus 5.5)

Scope given by the owner (R-80): backend and mobile; the CMS half of P4 moves to P9. Failing test first for each bug. Local branches, not pushed: be `fix/p4-membership-plans` (from `origin/main` `fc7f78c`), app `fix/p4-membership-plans` (from `origin/master` `932c4ac`).

**Rule (BUG-05).** A plan is visible to travellers when it is `ACTIVE`, not deactivated and public. It counts for a branch when it is for that branch or for every branch (`branchId` null). Discovery hides a branch with none (`discovery.service.js:128-130`), so the last one at an `ACTIVE` branch cannot be hidden, deactivated or deleted. One function on each side: be `membership-plan.service.js#isTravellerVisible`, app `membership_plans/domain/plan_visibility.dart#isLastVisiblePlan`. The server is the authority; the app only explains it earlier.

**Currency rule (BUG-09).** A plan has no currency column; its branch has one (GLB-02, tenant migration 019). A branch plan uses its branch's currency. A gym-wide plan uses the branch it is shown for, else the organization's first branch (the one a new branch copies its currency from, `gym.service.js` `_createBranchRecord`). Become a Host has no branch yet: the tenant payload carries `mainBranchCurrency`, and provisioning creates the first branch with that same value (`currency.utils.js#firstBranchCurrency`, PKR, R-80).

**Deploy.** Backend: `git pull`, `iisreset` (no migration, no env variable; `web.config` is watched, so the site restarts on pull anyway). Then check `https://api.gymsera.com/iisnode/index.html` answers 404. CMS and web: normal deploy, any order; the guards are harmless before the P7c backend is live (no `entitled` field = unchanged).

New: **NEW-184** (P2, decision) a billing-locked branch stays listed for travellers: discovery's `TRAVELLER_VISIBLE_BRANCH` (`discovery.service.js:14`) does not look at `billing_locked_at`, while `subscribe` refuses it (`subscription.service.js:212`, `branch_billing_locked`). A traveller finds the gym and then cannot book it. A lapsed tenant is not live at all, so today this affects the locked over-quota branches of a tenant whose plan still entitles. Decide whether locked branches leave discovery (`why-not-live` reports the lock as WARN until then). Also seen: 22 iisnode log files from another app ("Shah Alam Dairies") are tracked in `iisnode/` (commit `665dc55`, before the `.gitignore` entry); harmless content, remove in a cleanup commit.

## 14. Remaining risks and open decisions

> **Decisions recorded 2026-09-26.** The owner delegated these to the architect (Claude Code) and they are now **final for agents**. Agents apply them and do **not** stop to ask about them again. The owner may override any row at any time by editing it.

| # | Risk / decision | Owner | **DECIDED** |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-1 | IAP vs web-first sales channel per region (BILL-16) | Product | **Keep IAP in the apps.** The web sells nothing by card for now (see R-7). |
| R-2 | Member-grace length when a branch locks (CAP-01, §7.5.8) | Product | **DECIDED 2026-09-28: 7 days.** A gym's own members can still check in for 7 days after the branch locks. Config value `MEMBER_CHECKIN_GRACE_DAYS=7`. The owner approved the spec default. |
| R-3 | Over-quota grace before locking | Product | **DECIDED 2026-09-28: 7 days, shown to the host as a countdown banner.** Config value `OVERQUOTA_GRACE_DAYS=7`. The owner approved the spec default. |
| R-4 | Card at registration: setup mode vs charge + refund on rejection (FLOW-03) | Product + Finance | **DECIDED 2026-09-28: Setup mode: save the card and charge only when the application is approved** (not charge immediately and refund on rejection). No charge before approval, so nothing to refund. The owner approved the spec default. Web card payments are still OFF (R-7), so this takes effect only once they are turned on. |
| R-5 | Auto-approve additional organizations (FLOW-11) | Product + Trust | **Manual review stays.** |
| R-6 | Legacy `PlatformPackage` subscribers' migration timing (BILL-10) | Finance | **At their next renewal.** |
| R-7 | Stripe availability for the legal entity's country; merchant-of-record alternative (BILL-17) | Founder/Finance | **Stripe is not available for Pakistan-based companies** (the code is PKR / `Asia/Karachi` / CNIC / JazzCash). **Web card payments stay OFF.** Web signup offers bank transfer and pay-later only; plans are bought in the app (App Store / Play). Keep the Stripe code behind a feature flag `WEB_CARD_PAYMENTS_ENABLED=false`; don't delete it. A later web provider (a merchant of record such as Paddle, or a local gateway) plugs in behind the same catalog and provider abstraction. The owner confirms the entity's country. |
| R-8 | `TenantDbManager` at thousands of tenants: pool-per-server redesign (PERF-07) | Engineering | **Caps + LRU now**; revisit at 500 active tenants. |
| R-9 | Play upgrade/downgrade eligibility not verified on a production Play Console (documented open item) | Engineering | **Must pass the staging checklist before launch** (owner tests on a real device). |
| R-10 | Offline check-in queue (§11.4) | Product | **Not built; online only.** |
| R-11 | Data residency requirements in some countries (e.g. EU customers) | Legal | **Single region**; documented in the privacy policy. |
| R-12 | Everything in this document derives from docs and screen maps; the code may differ | Agent | Verify-first rule (§0.1). |
| R-13 | Mobile navigation refinements proposed in §2.3.1 and on the UI canvas (drawer → More hub, label renames, front-desk shell) | Owner | **Keep mobile as it is.** UX-03, UX-05, UX-16 and the mobile part of UX-04 are `DEFERRED (R-13)`. |
| R-14 | Web-only features with no mobile equivalent (CMS Trainers, web Account statement) and mobile-only traveler features on the web (checkout, wishlist) | Owner | **Keep as they are; no new work.** UX-25 is `DEFERRED (R-14)`. |
| R-15 | **Boost** (BILL-18). Code check: `gyms_era/lib/features/host/presentation/screens/boost_listing_screen.dart` shows Rs 1,500 / 4,500 / 8,000 tiers, and "Pay & Activate Boost" shows a success dialog **without charging anything**; the backend has no Boost code. | Product | **Boost is a paid product, but it is not built.** In hardening: replace the fake "Pay & Activate" with a disabled **"Coming soon"** state (a fake success screen is a store-review and trust risk). Building it later is a new feature: an IAP product in the one catalog, the same `/sync` + webhook pipeline (BILL-18 fix). Log it as NEW-BOOST in §12.13. |
| R-16 | Retention periods for account/tenant deletion (AUTH-07, FLOW-01, SEC-10) | Legal | **30-day undo window** after a deletion request. **Financial records** (payments, invoices, ledger, platform invoices) kept **6 years, anonymized** (Pakistan tax record rule; owner confirms with the accountant). **KYC files** deleted 90 days after rejection or tenant deletion. **Abandoned DRAFT applications** purged after 30 days. **Application logs** kept 90 days. |
| R-17 | Web-registration payment choices while R-7 is OFF | Product | Options shown: **Bank transfer** and **Pay later**. Pay-later grace (BILL-13, §7.5.11) = **14 days**, config value `PAY_LATER_GRACE_DAYS=14`. |
| R-18 | CMS Inbox (UX-23) | Product | **Out of scope.** Build the notifications bell + feed only. |
| R-19 | Database environment (owner statement, 2026-09-26) | Owner | The "production" database holds **test data only**, so agents may connect to it to investigate and to apply migrations. **Automated test suites still run only against a local/Docker MySQL**, because the harness creates and drops databases and would break the live apps the owner tests on. Real store/Stripe keys stay out of the code: sandbox only. |
| R-20 | Upgrade production MySQL 5.7 → 8.0/8.4 (5.7 is end-of-life; requested in Step 2.10 under R-15) | Infrastructure / Engineering | **Four-step upgrade plan**: 1. **Full backup**: Complete `mysqldump` (`--single-transaction --quick --routines --triggers --events`) of platform DB and all tenant databases. 2. **Restore test**: Restore into a staging / local MySQL 8.0/8.4 instance, run `mysqlcheck --check-upgrade`, verify collation compatibility (`utf8mb4_unicode_ci`), run full backend integration test suite. 3. **Maintenance window**: Announce scheduled downtime, stop API services (`docker compose down` / service pause) to guarantee zero in-flight writes, perform fresh final snapshot. 4. **Switchover & Verification**: Execute MySQL engine upgrade/container replacement, run startup schema verification check, run `run-tenant-migrations.js --dry-run` then execute, run smoke tests across endpoints, restart API services, verify monitoring. |
| R-21 | Should `GET /host/subscription/current` create a plan at all? (raised by NEW-15, 2026-09-28) | Owner | **DECIDED 2026-09-28: `GET /host/subscription/current` is read-only. It never creates a plan. The owner approved this.** The first plan comes only from onboarding (submission / approval, later the BILL-13 pay-later GRACE row). Implemented in §13 row NEW-15 / R-21. |
| R-22 | Pay-later grace period: days a host has to pay after approval before branches become non-entitling (BILL-13, §7.5.11; same value as R-17) | Owner | **DECIDED 2026-09-28: 14 days.** Config value `PAY_LATER_GRACE_DAYS=14`.<br>owner confirmed: yes (2026-09-29)<br>R-22 and R-17 are the same value, held in one setting (`PAY_LATER_GRACE_DAYS`). There is no second copy. |
| R-23 | Renewal webhooks for suspended tenants (NEW-26, Point 3) | Owner | **DECIDED 2026-09-29: Record what the provider reports on TenantSubscription for audit and billing history, but do not grant entitlement or unlock any branches/capacity while the tenant is SUSPENDED.** The webhook records the provider's transaction, dates, and amounts in `tenant_subscriptions` and logs the event, but skips capacity reconciliation, leaves branch locks intact, and does not alter the tenant's SUSPENDED status. |
| R-24 | Prompt 1F scope (raised 2026-09-30: the prompt was much bigger than described) | Owner | **DECIDED 2026-09-30: split.** Prompt 1F does SEC-03, SEC-07, SEC-09, RT-04 and the UX-12 plan. FLOW-02, SEC-10 and AUTH-07 each become their own prompt (1G, 1H, 1I in the playbook) and are verified again before work starts. |
| R-25 | Hard-coded DB credential fallback in tenant provisioning (`tenant-provisioning.service.js`: platform credentials, then `root` with an empty password) | Owner | **DECIDED 2026-09-30: remove it and fail loudly.** Provisioning uses only `TENANT_DB_ADMIN_USER` / `TENANT_DB_ADMIN_PASS` and stops with a clear error when they are missing or rejected (same approach as SEC-DB-FALLBACK). Done in Prompt 1F as its own commit, separate from FLOW-02. **Extended 2026-09-30 (owner approved):** the seed script `src/scripts/provision-seeded-tenants.js` follows the same rule (see §13 "R-25 (seed script)"). |
| R-26 | Google RTDN authentication cutover (SEC-03) | Owner | **DECIDED 2026-09-30: OIDC only.** The static `?token=` check is removed. RTDN is accepted only with a valid Google-signed OIDC token for the configured audience and service account. Deploy note: update the Pub/Sub push subscription (authentication on, same audience and service account) **before** this backend goes live. Otherwise RTDNs get 401 and Pub/Sub retries them for up to 7 days, and the daily sync keeps running. |
| R-27 | Private KYC storage and document protection (SEC-10) | Owner | **DECIDED 2026-10-01:** (1) Dedicated private bucket name `R2_KYC_BUCKET` reusing primary R2 credentials with S3 Server-Side Encryption (`AES256`). When R2 is not configured or in local dev/testing (`STORAGE_DRIVER=local`), fall back to a non-public local directory `storage/private/kyc/` outside public web roots (`public/uploads`). (2) Access to KYC documents is strictly gated by authenticated API endpoints (only the owning host and authorized platform admins) generating short-lived signed URLs (or authenticated streaming proxy if local storage). Every access is recorded in `PlatformAuditLog` (`action: KYC_DOCUMENT_ACCESSED`). (3) Admin views in CMS display an un-copyable visual watermark overlay (viewing admin email, IP/timestamp, "CONFIDENTIAL - AUDITED ACCESS"). (4) 90-day retention cron sweep automatically deletes KYC files from storage 90 days after tenant rejection or deletion, clearing `kycDocumentsJson` and logging `KYC_DOCUMENTS_PURGED` to `PlatformAuditLog`. (5) Full-stack implementation across backend, CMS admin viewer, web onboarding upload, and mobile host onboarding KYC upload. |
| R-28 | Account and tenant deletion (AUTH-07, Prompt 1I) and orphan databases (NEW-34) | Owner | **DECIDED 2026-10-02 (owner answered through the question tool):** (1) **Timeline.** A confirmed request (after re-authentication) takes effect at once: the user and every tenant they own go to `PENDING_DELETION` (all tenant/member access stops through the existing tenant status gate, the listing leaves discovery, sessions and device tokens are removed, members are notified). The **30-day undo window** (R-16) applies to host accounts **and** member-only accounts. At day 30 a sweep finishes the deletion (dry-run first, then apply). (2) **Financial records:** payments, invoices, ledger, platform invoices and tenant subscriptions are **kept 6 years, anonymized in place**. They only ever pointed at people by user id, so scrubbing the `users` row and the free-text fields anonymizes them. **The tenant database is NOT dropped and no automatic DROP exists.** A deleted tenant keeps its database, flagged `DELETED`; dropping it after year 6 is a manual owner step. (3) **Members of a deleted tenant:** told at request time; their active memberships are cancelled at day 30 (no automatic refunds); their own platform accounts are untouched; their receipts/history stay readable with the gym's business name kept; staff access is blocked at once by the tenant status and the assignments are revoked at day 30. (4) **Preflight:** a live Apple or Google subscription **blocks** deletion (409 with the store link, because only the user can cancel it); Stripe subscriptions are cancelled automatically at period end. Re-auth = current password, or a fresh Google/Apple token for social accounts (same check as SEC-13). (5) **Apple Sign-In revoke:** built behind configuration, no secrets in code (R-25 pattern). With no Apple key configured, deletion still completes and records `skipped`. The owner supplies the key later. (6) **NEW-34:** `rejectTenant` answers 409 while a live provisioning lease is held and marks the tenant's listing `INACTIVE`. Orphan databases are **reported only** (read-only listing); dropping one is a manual, explicit, per-database script run (`--drop <tenantCode> --confirm <dbName>`), never automatic. (7) **Scope:** backend, mobile delete-account flow and web privacy copy; the CMS only displays the new statuses. |

Mobile orange stays the brand colour on every surface; only the button colour changed. Source: `docs/design-tokens.json`. |

(2) A branch can be **paused**: hidden from travellers, no data loss, by whoever may change its settings (owner, organization admin, manager); always allowed. (3) Deleting a branch, or an organization (= deleting all of its branches in one transaction, all or nothing), is **refused** with 409, a code, counts and reasons while there are paid-up active memberships, pending or cash-collected payments, or upcoming bookings. **No refund logic**: the host settles them or waits, or pauses instead. (4) Payment approval is refused at a branch that is not ACTIVE. (5) The platform membership copy follows a delete. (6) Deleting the last branch still deletes the organization; the confirm screen says so; the copy says close, not "permanently". (7) Deleting the last branch or an organization never touches the store subscription; the host is told it still bills and where to cancel. The server never cancels it. (8) **Move branch is removed**, not frozen: no route, no service, no `moveBranches` strategy, no `ENABLE_BRANCH_MOVE`. **Post-launch (NEW-166):** a "transfer branch" may come back only with a row lock and a transaction, a capacity-ledger record, re-linking of plans and `UserGymMembership` / saved gyms to the target organization, a rule for reviews (follow the branch or stay), and the password. Implemented in §13 "Phase 7 Prompt P5b". |

(2) EXPIRED cuts capacity like the other ended states; the nightly capacity pass covers tenants with no entitling plan. (3) The nightly store re-check also covers store rows that became EXPIRED in the last 7 days. (4) Apple: Production first, Sandbox on transaction-not-found, the notification's environment first (TestFlight and App Review purchases verify). (5) `GET /host/subscription/current` returns the latest non-entitling row (status, reason, store link) instead of 404; the app shows "Plan ended", "On hold" or "Refunded" with the right action. (6) An Apple refund reversal makes the same period entitled again. (7) The host gets a push and an email on hold, refund, downgrade and expiry; expiry has its own template. (8) NEW-163: booking refuses a hidden plan or another branch's plan. (9) The daily job and the inbox retry must not depend on the iisnode process being alive (Task Scheduler). Added the same day: a billing lock and a host pause (P5b) are separate states. P3: Apple certificate OID checks (NEW-177), API docs (NEW-183). Implemented in §13 "Phase 7 Prompt P7c". |

**P1 owner and date (§17.2).** Recorded above as R-39 to R-62 from [`P1_OWNER_DATE.md`](P1_OWNER_DATE.md) (filled by the owner, merged in #147). R-63 to R-69 record the §17 criteria accepted as launch gaps, each with a date. R-72 to R-78 are the Phase 7 owner decisions D1 to D7 (`GYMSERA_PRODUCTION_FIX_PROMPTS.md` §1), recorded 2026-10-26.

**Rejected alternative (recorded so it isn't reopened by accident):** replacing `reservedSlots` with purely derived capacity (`available = entitled − activeBranches`). This would remove the slot-donor mechanism and ledger drift, but the capacity architecture is **locked**. All capacity defects are fixed inside the locked model (CAP-01…08). Revisit only if CAP-07 or OBS-06 shows recurring drift that the outbox cannot eliminate.

## 15. Testing matrix

**Tooling:**
- Backend: Jest + Supertest, plus a real MySQL via Testcontainers (platform DB + 2 tenant DBs).
- Flutter: `flutter_test` (unit/widget), golden tests, `integration_test`, `patrol` for native dialogs.
- Web: Vitest/Jest + React Testing Library; Playwright for E2E; axe for accessibility; Lighthouse CI.
- Billing: StoreKit Testing in Xcode + App Store sandbox; Google Play license testers and test cards; Stripe test mode + **test clocks**.

| Layer | What | Must cover | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Unit (backend)** | Services with fakes | Capacity math, state machines (subscription, membership, approval), money arithmetic, tier/level rules, error mapping | ≥ 90% line coverage on `services/billing`, `services/capacity`, `services/payments`, `approval.service.js` |
| **Integration (backend + real MySQL)** | Services + both DBs | Transactions, outbox, idempotency middleware, `reconcileCapacity`, `auditCapacity`, provisioning resume | Every P0 issue has ≥ 1 test |
| **API contract** | Every route | Envelope, error codes, validation (unknown fields rejected), pagination, ETag | Generated from the route list; 100% of routes |
| **Cross-client parity** | Same flow driven from the mobile integration test and the CMS/web E2E | Same endpoint, same request shape, same resulting data; the same person's effective permissions identical in app and CMS | Every ✅ row in §3.3 has one parity test |
| **Permission matrix** | Every mutating route × persona (Owner, Org admin, Manager, Front desk, Trainer, Cleaner, Member, Other-tenant owner, Admin sub-roles, Anonymous) | Allowed → 2xx, not allowed → 403/404, cross-tenant → 404 | 100% of mutating routes; fails CI on any unexpected 2xx |
| **Database** | Migrations | Up/down on an empty DB and on a prod-like snapshot; tenant migration runner resume; index presence | Runs on every PR touching models |
| **Concurrency** | Parallel requests | 20× `createBranch` at capacity; delete+restore race; donor-slot race; 50× invoice numbering; double refund; double approve; double webhook | Deterministic assertions |
| **Idempotency** | Replays | Same key → same response; different body → 422; in-flight → 409; app restart with persisted key | All mutations in the REL-01 list |
| **Billing** | Fixtures per provider notification type + sandbox runs | Purchase, restore (same/other tenant), renewal, price increase, upgrade, deferred downgrade, cancel, grace, hold, pause, expiry, refund/revoke, cross-provider, duplicate billing, out-of-order events, pending purchase, server-side ack | Fixture suite in CI; the sandbox checklist is manual per release |
| **Capacity property test** | Random operation sequences | Invariant holds; no empty ACTIVE org; ledger replay equals the stored slots | 1,000 random sequences per CI run |
| **Flutter unit/widget** | Notifiers + screens | §2.5 states for each screen; busy states; PermissionGate per persona; billing notifier (pending, stale transaction, success match); provider invalidation matrix §5.3 | Every screen with data has state tests |
| **Flutter golden** | Key screens | Light/dark, 200% text, `ar` RTL, 320-px width | Goldens reviewed on change |
| **Flutter integration** | On emulator/simulator | Login → bootstrap → create branch → 403 → upsell (fake store) → replay; create org → Listings visible (FLOW-05); logout clears everything | Nightly |
| **Web component** | CMS/web | States, forms, `PermissionGate`, org switcher | PR |
| **E2E (Playwright)** | Web flows | Host registration (bank / later / card with Stripe test mode); admin approve (with a provisioning-failure injection); host CMS enroll + record payment + close day; member portal views only own data | Nightly + pre-release |
| **Realtime** | Socket server + client | Auth on connect, room authorization, token refresh, reconnect replay, no duplicate delivery, listener cleanup | PR |
| **Notifications** | FCM (emulated) + device tests | Register, refresh, logout removal, tap routing with context switch, foreground banner | Pre-release on real devices |
| **Failure recovery** | Fault injection | Kill between tenant commit and platform effect; webhook processor crash; provisioning crash; client timeout mid-payment | Each recovery path in §11.3 |
| **Performance** | k6 / Lighthouse CI / Flutter traces | §10.1 budgets; 1,000-tenant pool soak; discovery 200 RPS | Pre-release; budgets fail CI when exceeded |
| **Security** | SAST, dependency audit, secret scan, DAST (OWASP ZAP) on staging, manual IDOR review | SEC-* | No high/critical findings open at release |
| **Accessibility** | axe (web), Flutter semantics tests, manual screen reader pass (TalkBack/VoiceOver) | §2.11 | No critical violations |
| **Redis independence** | Full backend test suite with `DISABLE_REDIS=true` | All tests pass with Redis disabled; distributed lock MySQL fallbacks and in-process auth caching operate cleanly without Redis | 100% test pass rate with Redis off |

---

## 16. Production deployment checklist


### Infrastructure
- [ ] Separate staging and production: databases, buckets, keys, store apps (Apple/Google test tracks), Stripe accounts.
- [ ] MySQL: automated backups for the platform DB **and every tenant DB**, with point-in-time recovery; a **restore drill** performed and timed.
- [ ] `max_connections` sized for the pool caps (PERF-07); a read replica for reports.
- [ ] Redis (rate limits, caches, Socket.IO adapter, job locks).
- [ ] Object storage + CDN with image variants; private bucket for KYC.
- [ ] TLS everywhere; HSTS; security headers (SEC-18). *(5A-4: both Next.js apps and the API send them; confirm the host / Cloudflare does not add conflicting ones; enforce the Report-Only CSP.)*
- [ ] Secrets in a secret manager / KMS; the key for `connectionStringEncrypted` has a documented rotation procedure. *(5A-4: rotation script and runbook exist, `docs/runbooks/secrets-rotation.md`; the secret manager itself and rotating any live demo key are owner actions; gitleaks runs on every change.)*

### Backend
- [ ] All migrations applied; the tenant migration runner reports 100% of tenants at the target `schemaVersion`.
- [ ] Cron and sweeps running with distributed locks (REL-03): billing reconciliation, voided purchases, outbox, BillingEvent retry, capacity audit, membership expiry, ledger reminders, DRAFT purge.
- [ ] Webhook endpoints registered and signature-verified:
  - [ ] Apple App Store Server Notifications V2 (production + sandbox URLs)
  - [ ] Google RTDN Pub/Sub push
  - [ ] Stripe
- [ ] Rate limits on. *(5A-4: per-route limits are in memory, single instance; check `trust proxy` / the client IP the API sees behind IIS and Cloudflare before relying on per-IP limits.)*
- [ ] Graceful shutdown configured (REL-05).
- [ ] `GET /health` (liveness) and `/ready` (DB, Redis) wired to the load balancer.

### Billing
- [ ] `BillingPlan` tiers mapped to live iOS product IDs, Android product + base plan IDs, and Stripe price IDs per currency; the verifier (BILL-15) shows no mismatch.
- [ ] Subscription groups (Apple) and base plans (Google) ordered so that upgrade/downgrade semantics are correct.
- [ ] Sandbox checklist passed on real devices for every flow in §7.5 (including R-9).
- [ ] Refund policy page matches the actual behaviour.

### Before launch: real store sandbox test
These cannot be proven with the fakes in the test suite (Prompt 1B). Each needs a real sandbox purchase on a
device, and the result recorded in §13:
- [ ] **Apple downgrade timing:** an in-group downgrade keeps the current product until renewal; the server shows it
  only as `pendingChange` (from `renewalInfo.autoRenewProductId`) and switches `branchCount` at the renewal (BILL-03).
- [ ] **Google deferred replacement token timing:** with `ReplacementMode.deferred`, when Play issues the new purchase
  token (at purchase or at renewal), what `startTime` it carries, and that the old plan stays until the switch (BILL-03).
- [ ] **Play `recurringPrice` vs the charged amount:** after a sandbox price change, the stored `amount`/`currency` match
  the order Google actually charged (BILL-05).
- [ ] **Google pending → completed sequence:** a "slow test card" purchase shows "Payment pending", grants nothing, and
  the plan appears only after Google's completion notification (BILL-08).

### Clients
- [ ] Flutter release builds with obfuscation + symbol upload; `minAppVersion` enforcement in bootstrap (force-update screen).
- [ ] Universal Links / App Links verified (`apple-app-site-association`, `assetlinks.json`).
- [ ] Push: APNs key uploaded to FCM; Android notification channels defined.
- [ ] Web: ISR revalidation tags wired; Lighthouse CI budgets green; CSP enforced (report-only first, then enforce).
- [ ] Store listings: privacy nutrition labels / Data Safety form match the actual data use; account-deletion path is discoverable (AUTH-07).

- [ ] **Mobile build env guard (app `5672417`, branch `feat/release-app-env-guard`):** release/profile builds fail to compile unless `APP_ENV` is passed. Production build commands: `flutter build ipa --release --dart-define=APP_ENV=production` (iOS / TestFlight / App Store) and `flutter build appbundle --release --dart-define=APP_ENV=production` (Android). Staging builds: same with `APP_ENV=staging`. After installing a build, confirm it talks to `api.gymsera.com` (production) or `apistaging.gymsera.com`; Xcode Cloud / CI / Codemagic scripts must pass the define too.

### Observability and operations
- [ ] Dashboards: API, DB, pools, billing, capacity, realtime, FCM. *(5A-3: the data is there at `GET /metrics` behind `METRICS_KEY`; no scraper or dashboard exists yet.)*
- [ ] Alerts with runbooks (OBS-09); on-call rota defined. *(5A-3: six alert codes with runbooks in `docs/runbooks/`, in-app and Sentry delivery; the on-call rota is still to be defined.)*
- [ ] Error tracking receiving events from all 4 apps with release tags. *(5A-3: backend only, inert until `SENTRY_DSN` is set; CMS, web and mobile are PR9.)*
- [ ] Log redaction verified on production-like traffic (SEC-07).

### Release process
- [ ] **Deploy prerequisite (BILL-09 / p017):** Execute platform migration `p017_tenant_subscriptions_duplicate_billing` (`node src/scripts/run-platform-migrations.js`) on the database BEFORE deploying code. `TenantSubscription.model.js` declares `duplicate_billing`; without the column, queries against `TenantSubscription` will fail with `Unknown column 'duplicate_billing' in 'field list'`.
- [ ] **Distributed lock safety (REL-03):** MySQL `GET_LOCK(key, 0)` fallback in `src/utils/distributed-lock.js` operates with timeout 0 (non-blocking). When Redis is unavailable, concurrent background jobs fail fast instead of blocking MySQL connection pool threads indefinitely. Note: MySQL `GET_LOCK` fallback has no TTL: a hung (not crashed) cron job keeps the lock until the connection drops or IIS recycles the process.
- [ ] Feature flags for risky changes (CAP-01 locking, BILL-10 catalog freeze, BILL-03 deferred downgrade), so they can be turned off without a deploy.
- [ ] Staged rollout: Play staged %, App Store phased release; web canary.
- [ ] Rollback plan per system: previous container image, previous app build still compatible with the API (API changes are additive only).
- [ ] Data migrations reversible (expand/contract).

### Before adding real data
Both items are deferred (recorded 2026-09-28): production holds test data only (R-19), and the owner plans a
full database wipe before real customers go live. Do them at that point, not before.
- [ ] Check whether the seeded platform-admin accounts (`src/seeders/seed.js`) exist in the production database.
  If they do, delete them or change their passwords before any real gym signs up.
- [ ] Confirm the `noreply@gymsera.com` mailbox password has been changed (it was committed to git history on
  2026-08-31), and that git history is eventually cleaned or repo access is reviewed.

---

## 17. Final production-readiness criteria

GymsEra is production-ready **only when every item below is true and backed by evidence**: a test run link, a staging verification record, or a dashboard. Reading the code is not evidence.

1. **Zero open P0 issues** in §12. Each has status `DONE` (with a test) or `NOT REPRODUCED` (with file/line proof) in §13.
2. **All P1 issues** are `DONE` or explicitly `DEFERRED` with an owner and date in §14.
3. **Permission matrix suite** is green: no mutating endpoint returns 2xx for a persona that shouldn't have access, and there is no cross-tenant access.
4. **Billing sandbox checklist** passed on real iOS and Android devices and in Stripe test mode, for every flow in §7.5, within the last 14 days before release.
5. **Capacity:**
   - The property test is green.
   - `auditCapacity` across all staging tenants shows **zero drift** and **zero empty ACTIVE organizations**.
   - The concurrency tests are green.
6. **Money:**
   - Idempotency, ledger immutability, daily close, refund, and invoice-sequence tests are green.
   - Staging reconciliation (sum of payments = sum of ledger per day per branch) is exact.
7. **Recovery:** every §11.3 scenario was fault-injected in staging and recovered without manual database edits.
8. **Performance budgets** (§10.1) met on the reference device/network and in load tests.
9. **Security:**
   - No high or critical findings open from SAST, dependency audit, secret scan, and DAST.
   - Webhook signature tests green.
   - Log redaction verified.
10. **Global:**
    - The app works in at least one RTL locale and one non-PKR currency end to end.
    - Timezone tests pass across a DST boundary.
11. **Accessibility:** no critical axe violations; a screen reader pass completed on the host core flow (login → Today → check-in → record payment).
12. **Operations:**
    - Dashboards and alerts are live.
    - The restore drill was completed.
    - Runbooks exist for billing, capacity drift, provisioning failure, and webhook backlog.

**Accepted launch gaps (owner decision 2026-10-22, §14 R-63 to R-69).** Criteria 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12 may be unmet at launch, each with a date by which it must be closed: 3 and 5 and 10 and 11 by 2027-01-31, 7 and 12 by 2026-12-31, 8 by 2027-01-15. Criteria 1, 2, 4, 6 and 9 are **not** accepted gaps and still block launch.

---

## Appendix A — Glossary

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Tenant | One host account: one owner, one GymsEra entitlement |
| Organization | A brand a tenant runs (`GymListing`); free, never empty |
| Branch | A physical location; consumes capacity |
| `maxBranches` | Branches the current entitlement covers |
| `reservedSlots` | Paid but unbuilt capacity parked on an organization |
| Entitlement | What the tenant may use right now, from the one ACTIVE/GRACE `TenantSubscription` row |
| `billingLock` | Branch restricted because the plan no longer covers it; not a lifecycle status |
| Membership | A member's purchase of a branch's membership plan (`MemberSubscription`) |
| Tier | Permission level per module: NONE/VIEW/REQUEST/APPROVE/DIRECT/FULL |
| Outbox | Tenant-DB table that makes platform-DB side effects durable |
| BillingEvent | Inbox row per provider webhook/notification |

## Appendix B — Agent quick-start

0. Read `AGENT_HANDOFF.md` (next to this file). If a task is in progress, continue it from its "Next action" instead of starting something new.
1. Read §0 (especially §0.5: mobile is the reference), §3.3 (parity matrix), §8.3 (Team & Access), then §12.1–§12.2 (the P0 money issues).
2. For each P0: locate the code → confirm or refute → write the failing test → fix using the listed reuse → test green → log it in §13.
3. After Phase 1, re-run the whole §15 suite and update §14.
4. Never mark §17 criteria as met without a linked test run or staging record.
